<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We determine the unassisted classical capacity of every qubit generalized amplitude-damping channel, for all damping strengths and thermal occupations. It equals the one-shot Holevo capacity and is given by a one-variable maximum. A binary pure-state ensemble attains the one-use optimum, and its independent products attain the optimum at every block length. We also prove that one-shot Holevo capacity, minimum output entropy, and regularized unassisted classical capacity are additive under tensor product with every finite-dimensional completely positive trace-preserving partner channel.

- **stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For all $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$ and every integer $n\ge1$, $$\label{eq:main} \chi(\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu}^{\otimes n}) =n\chi(\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu}) =\frac n{\ln2}\max_{0\le p\le1} \{h((1-\gamma)p+\gamma\nu)-g(v_{\gamma,\nu}(p))\},$$ where $$\label{eq:v-main} v_{\gamma,\nu}(p) =\gamma\nu(1-\nu)+\gamma(1-\gamma)(p-\nu)^2.$$ In particular, $C(\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu})=\chi(\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu})$. If $p$ maximizes <a href="#eq:main" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main">[eq:main]</a>, independent products of the two equiprobable signals $$\label{eq:pair} \phi_\pm=\sqrt{1-p}\,\lvert 0\rangle\ \pm\sqrt p\,\lvert 1\rangle$$ attain the Holevo information at every block length.
  - Explanation: The scalar population optimization gives the unassisted classical capacity, and independent products of the opposite-phase pair attain every finite-block Holevo optimum.
- **stmt-cor-partner-additivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-partner-additivity)): Let $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, put $\mathcal A=\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu}$, and let $\mathcal N:\mathcal B(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{in}})\to \mathcal B(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{out}})$ be completely positive and trace preserving, where $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{in}}$ and $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{out}}$ are nonzero finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces. Then $$\begin{aligned} \chi(\mathcal A\otimes\mathcal N) &=\chi(\mathcal A)+\chi(\mathcal N),\label{eq:partner-holevo}\\ S_{\min}(\mathcal A\otimes\mathcal N) &=S_{\min}(\mathcal A)+S_{\min}(\mathcal N),\label{eq:partner-min}\\ C(\mathcal A\otimes\mathcal N) &=C(\mathcal A)+C(\mathcal N).\label{eq:partner-capacity} \end{aligned}$$ Here $\chi$ is the unconstrained one-shot Holevo capacity in <a href="#eq:holevo" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:holevo">[eq:holevo]</a>, and $C$ is the regularized unassisted classical capacity in <a href="#eq:regularization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:regularization">[eq:regularization]</a>.
  - Explanation: Holevo capacity, minimum output entropy, and unassisted classical capacity add when the damping channel is paired with any finite-dimensional channel satisfying the stated hypotheses. The partner capacity retains its regularization.
- **stmt-thm-block** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-block)): Let $A,B,C\in\mathbb C^{d\times e}$, and put $$M=\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\ C&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad x=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(AA^*),\quad y=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(BB^*),\quad z=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(CC^*).$$ If $x+y+z=1$, then $$\label{eq:block} \mathcal S(MM^*)\ge x\mathcal S(AA^*/x)+y\mathcal S(BB^*/y)+z\mathcal S(CC^*/z)+g(yz),$$ where each term with zero weight is omitted.
  - Explanation: The normalized rectangular block factor has an entropy lower bound retaining both the internal block entropies and the scalar determinant contribution.
- **stmt-lem-integral** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-integral)): If $U,V\ge0$ have equal trace, with possibly different sizes, then $$\label{eq:block-entropy-integral} \mathcal S(U)-\mathcal S(V) =\int_0^\infty \bigl[\ln\det(I+rU)-\ln\det(I+rV)\bigr]\frac{\,\mathrm dr}{r^2}.$$ The integral converges absolutely.
  - Explanation: Equal trace permits an absolutely convergent log-determinant representation of the entropy difference, even for different matrix sizes.
- **stmt-lem-logdet** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-logdet)): For each fixed $Z\in\mathbb C^{N\times N}$, the function $$\label{eq:block-G} G_Z(D,E)=\ln\det(D+ZE^{-1}Z^*)-\ln\det D, \qquad D,E>0,$$ is jointly convex on pairs of positive definite Hermitian matrices.
  - Explanation: The fixed-cross-matrix log-determinant difference is jointly convex on positive definite Hermitian pairs.
- **stmt-lem-scalar** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar)): The function $F$ is positively homogeneous of degree one and concave on the probability simplex. For every finite collection of nonnegative triples, $$\label{eq:block-superadditive} \sum_i F(x_i,y_i,z_i) \le F\!\left(\sum_i x_i,\sum_i y_i,\sum_i z_i\right).$$
  - Explanation: The scalar deficit is homogeneous and concave, yielding superadditivity over nonnegative triples.
- **stmt-lem-binary-spectrum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-spectrum)): The function $s\mapsto g(s^2)$ is nondecreasing and convex on $[0,1/2]$. Consequently, $g$ is nondecreasing on $[0,1/4]$.
  - Explanation: The entropy as a function of the square root of its determinant is convex and nondecreasing; determinant entropy is nondecreasing.
- **stmt-thm-thermal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-thermal)): Let $X,Y$ be complex matrices of the same rectangular shape with $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(XX^*)=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(YY^*)=1$. Suppose $\alpha,\beta,\eta,\delta\ge0$ sum to one and $$\label{eq:thermal-condition} \alpha\delta-\beta\eta=K^2,\qquad K\ge0.$$ Set $$\label{eq:thermal-state} T=\begin{pmatrix} \alpha XX^*+\beta YY^*&KXY^*\\ KYX^*&\eta XX^*+\delta YY^* \end{pmatrix},\qquad U_0=\alpha+\beta,\quad U_1=\eta+\delta,$$ and $u=U_0U_1-K^2$. Then $T$ is a state, $0\le u\le1/4$, and $$\label{eq:thermal-bound} \mathcal S(T)\ge(\alpha+\eta)\mathcal S(XX^*) +(\beta+\delta)\mathcal S(YY^*)+g(u).$$
  - Explanation: The normalized thermal block state obeys an entropy lower bound preserving the Gram-factor entropies and the scalar determinant term.
- **stmt-lem-e-convex** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-e-convex)): For every $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, the function $p\mapsto g(\gamma\nu(1-\nu)+\gamma(1-\gamma)(p-\nu)^2)$ is convex on $[0,1]$.
  - Explanation: The pure-input output entropy is convex in the input excitation population throughout the parameter square.
- **stmt-lem-partner-branch** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-partner-branch)): Let $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, put $\mathcal A=\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu}$ and $e(p)=g(v_{\gamma,\nu}(p))$, and let $\mathcal N:\mathcal B(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{in}})\to \mathcal B(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{out}})$ be completely positive and trace preserving on nonzero finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces. For a unit vector $\psi\in\mathbb C^2\otimes\mathcal H_{\mathrm{in}}$, write $$\psi=\sqrt{1-p}\,\lvert 0\rangle\otimes\psi_0 +\sqrt p\,\lvert 1\rangle\otimes\psi_1, \qquad 0\le p\le1,$$ where each $\psi_i$ is a unit vector and a zero-weight branch may be chosen arbitrarily. Then $$\label{eq:partner-branch} \begin{split} \mathcal S\bigl((\mathcal A\otimes\mathcal N)(\lvert \psi\rangle\langle \psi\rvert)\bigr) &\ge e(p)+(1-p)\mathcal S\bigl(\mathcal N(\lvert \psi_0\rangle\langle \psi_0\rvert)\bigr)\\ &\quad+p\mathcal S\bigl(\mathcal N(\lvert \psi_1\rangle\langle \psi_1\rvert)\bigr). \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: The joint output entropy retains the damping contribution and the weighted conditional partner entropies for arbitrary branch overlap.
- **stmt-prop-pure-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-bound)): For every $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, put $\mathcal A=\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu}$ and $e(p)=g(v_{\gamma,\nu}(p))$. For every integer $n\ge1$ and every unit vector $\psi$ on $n$ qubits, let $p_j$ be the probability of outcome $1$ at site $j$ in the computational basis. Then $$\label{eq:pure-bound} \mathcal S\bigl(\mathcal A^{\otimes n}(\lvert \psi\rangle\langle \psi\rvert)\bigr) \ge\sum_{j=1}^n e(p_j).$$
  - Explanation: The output entropy of an arbitrary pure block input is bounded below by the sum of the population-dependent site contributions.
- **stmt-cor-mixed-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-mixed-bound)): For every $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, integer $n\ge1$, and density matrix $\rho$ on $(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes n}$, let $p_j$ be its excitation probability at site $j$. Then $$\mathcal S\bigl(\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu}^{\otimes n}(\rho)\bigr) \ge\sum_{j=1}^n g(v_{\gamma,\nu}(p_j)).$$
  - Explanation: The same population-dependent entropy lower bound holds for mixed block inputs.
- **stmt-cor-ordinary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-ordinary)): For $\gamma\in[0,1]$, let $\mathcal A_\gamma$ be the qubit channel with Kraus operators $$K_0=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(1,\sqrt{1-\gamma}),\qquad K_1=\sqrt\gamma\,\lvert 0\rangle\langle 1\rvert.$$ For every integer $n\ge1$, $$\chi(\mathcal A_\gamma^{\otimes n})=n C(\mathcal A_\gamma) =\frac n{\ln2}\max_{0\le p\le1} \{h((1-\gamma)p)-g(\gamma(1-\gamma)p^2)\}.$$
  - Explanation: Ordinary amplitude damping is the zero-occupation specialization of the capacity formula.
- **stmt-cor-unital** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-unital)): For every $\gamma\in[0,1]$, $$C(\mathcal A_{\gamma,1/2}) =1-\frac1{\ln2}g(\gamma/4) =1-\frac1{\ln2}h\!\left(\frac{1+\sqrt{1-\gamma}}2\right).$$ The pair $(\lvert 0\rangle\pm\lvert 1\rangle)/\sqrt2$ attains the one-use Holevo information, and its independent products attain every finite-power Holevo optimum.
  - Explanation: At the unital midpoint the symmetric opposite-phase pair attains the stated closed capacity formula and every finite-power optimum.

Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi(\\mathcal A\\otimes\\mathcal N)\n &=\\chi(\\mathcal A)+\\chi(\\mathcal N),\\label{eq:partner-holevo}\\\\\n S_{\\min}(\\mathcal A\\otimes\\mathcal N)\n &=S_{\\min}(\\mathcal A)+S_{\\min}(\\mathcal N),\\label{eq:partner-min}\\\\\n C(\\mathcal A\\otimes\\mathcal N)\n &=C(\\mathcal A)+C(\\mathcal N).\\label{eq:partner-capacity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\chi$ is the unconstrained one-shot Holevo capacity in <a href=\"#eq:holevo\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:holevo\">[eq:holevo]</a>, and $C$ is the regularized unassisted classical capacity in <a href=\"#eq:regularization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:regularization\">[eq:regularization]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-276.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $A,B,C\\in\\mathbb C^{d\\times e}$, and put $$M=\\begin{pmatrix}A&B\\\\ C&0\\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n x=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(AA^*),\\quad y=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(BB^*),\\quad z=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(CC^*).$$ If $x+y+z=1$, then $$\\label{eq:block}\n \\mathcal S(MM^*)\\ge\n x\\mathcal S(AA^*/x)+y\\mathcal S(BB^*/y)+z\\mathcal S(CC^*/z)+g(yz),$$ where each term with zero weight is omitted.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-thermal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,Y$ be complex matrices of the same rectangular shape with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(XX^*)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}(YY^*)=1$. Suppose $\\alpha,\\beta,\\eta,\\delta\\ge0$ sum to one and $$\\label{eq:thermal-condition}\n \\alpha\\delta-\\beta\\eta=K^2,\\qquad K\\ge0.$$ Set $$\\label{eq:thermal-state}\n T=\\begin{pmatrix}\n \\alpha XX^*+\\beta YY^*&KXY^*\\\\\n KYX^*&\\eta XX^*+\\delta YY^*\n \\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n U_0=\\alpha+\\beta,\\quad U_1=\\eta+\\delta,$$ and $u=U_0U_1-K^2$. Then $T$ is a state, $0\\le u\\le1/4$, and $$\\label{eq:thermal-bound}\n \\mathcal S(T)\\ge(\\alpha+\\eta)\\mathcal S(XX^*)\n             +(\\beta+\\delta)\\mathcal S(YY^*)+g(u).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-276.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\gamma,\\nu\\in[0,1]$, put $\\mathcal A=\\mathcal A_{\\gamma,\\nu}$ and $e(p)=g(v_{\\gamma,\\nu}(p))$, and let $\\mathcal N:\\mathcal B(\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm{in}})\\to\n\\mathcal B(\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm{out}})$ be completely positive and trace preserving on nonzero finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces. For a unit vector $\\psi\\in\\mathbb C^2\\otimes\\mathcal H_{\\mathrm{in}}$, write $$\\psi=\\sqrt{1-p}\\,\\lvert 0\\rangle\\otimes\\psi_0\n       +\\sqrt p\\,\\lvert 1\\rangle\\otimes\\psi_1,\n \\qquad 0\\le p\\le1,$$ where each $\\psi_i$ is a unit vector and a zero-weight branch may be chosen arbitrarily. Then $$\\label{eq:partner-branch}\n \\begin{split}\n \\mathcal S\\bigl((\\mathcal A\\otimes\\mathcal N)(\\lvert \\psi\\rangle\\langle \\psi\\rvert)\\bigr)\n &\\ge e(p)+(1-p)\\mathcal S\\bigl(\\mathcal N(\\lvert \\psi_0\\rangle\\langle \\psi_0\\rvert)\\bigr)\\\\\n &\\quad+p\\mathcal S\\bigl(\\mathcal N(\\lvert \\psi_1\\rangle\\langle \\psi_1\\rvert)\\bigr).\n \\end{split}$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Matrix entropy** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a positive semidefinite matrix, not necessarily normalized, write $$\mathcal S(P)=-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(P\ln P),\qquad 0\ln0=0.$$
- **Generalized amplitude-damping channel** ([source](#eq:channel)): For $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, define the generalized amplitude-damping channel by $$\label{eq:channel} \mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu} \begin{pmatrix}1-q&z\\\overline z&q\end{pmatrix} =\begin{pmatrix}1-t&\sqrt{1-\gamma}\,z\\ \sqrt{1-\gamma}\,\overline z&t\end{pmatrix}, \qquad t=(1-\gamma)q+\gamma\nu.$$
- **Holevo capacity** ([source](#eq:holevo)): We measure Holevo information and capacity in *bits*: $$\label{eq:holevo} \chi(\mathcal N)=\frac1{\ln2} \sup_{\{q_a,\rho_a\}} \left\{\mathcal S\!\left(\sum_aq_a\mathcal N(\rho_a)\right) -\sum_aq_a\mathcal S\bigl(\mathcal N(\rho_a)\bigr)\right\}.$$
- **Scalar entropy deficit** ([source](#eq:block-F)): For $x,y,z\ge0$, define $$\begin{aligned} m&=x+y+z,\qquad \lambda_\pm=\frac{m\pm\sqrt{m^2-4yz}}2, \label{eq:block-lambda}\\ F(x,y,z)&=-x\ln x-y\ln y-z\ln z +\lambda_+\ln\lambda_++\lambda_-\ln\lambda_-. \label{eq:block-F} \end{aligned}$$
- **Pure-input determinant and entropy** ([source](#eq:single-entropy)): For $p\in[0,1]$, define $$\label{eq:single-entropy} \begin{split} v(p)&=(a(1-p)+bp)(c(1-p)+dp)-k^2p(1-p)\\ &=ac(1-p)^2+bdp^2+2bcp(1-p),\qquad e(p)=g(v(p)). \end{split}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| output-determinant | Pure-input output determinant | = | $\gamma\nu(1-\nu)+\gamma(1-\gamma)(p-\nu)^2$ | Damping, occupation, and input population in the unit interval. |
| output-population | Output excited-state population | = | $(1-\gamma)q+\gamma\nu$ | Input population and both channel parameters in the unit interval. |
| coherence-multiplier | Coherence multiplier | = | $\sqrt{1-\gamma}$ | Damping strength in the unit interval. |
| determinant-domain | Qubit determinant upper bound | ≤ | $\frac14$ | Nonnegative determinant in the stated interval. |
| thermal-determinant-bound | Thermal scalar determinant upper bound | ≤ | $1/4$ | Nonnegative normalized coefficients and the stated cross-coefficient identity. |
| minimum-output-entropy | Minimum output entropy in nats | = | $g\bigl(\gamma\nu(1-\nu)\bigr)$ | Damping and stationary occupation in the unit interval. |
| unital-capacity | Unital midpoint capacity in bits | = | $1-\frac1{\ln2}g(\gamma/4)$ | Stationary occupation at the midpoint and damping in the unit interval. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **integral** [claim](#stmt-lem-integral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integral): Integrate log-determinant differences using equal-trace endpoint cancellation. -> Entropy integral representation.
- **logdet** [claim](#stmt-lem-logdet) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-logdet): Use the skew-adjoint perturbation contraction to bound the directional second derivative. -> Joint convexity.
- **scalar** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar): Integrate scalar convexity and use homogeneity and continuity. -> Scalar concavity and superadditivity.
- **block** [claim](#stmt-thm-block) / [proof](#sec:block): Apply a Schur complement, simultaneous sign averaging, scalar superadditivity, and zero padding. -> Zero-block entropy lower bound.
- **spectrum** [claim](#stmt-lem-binary-spectrum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-binary-spectrum): Differentiate in the interior and extend by continuity. -> Monotonicity and square-root convexity.
- **thermal** [claim](#stmt-thm-thermal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-thermal): Mix zero-block Gram factorizations sharing the scalar determinant term; handle vanishing determinant separately. -> Thermal entropy lower bound.
- **population** [claim](#stmt-lem-e-convex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-e-convex): Compose a convex affine-vector norm with a nondecreasing convex entropy function. -> Convex pure-input output entropy.
- **branch** [claim](#stmt-lem-partner-branch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-partner-branch): Form rectangular Kraus-column matrices preserving the branch cross operator. -> Population-dependent partner branch bound.
- **iteration** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-bound): Induct on sites and combine conditional populations by convexity. -> Population-dependent block entropy lower bound.
- **mixed** [claim](#stmt-cor-mixed-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-mixed-bound): Use a finite pure-state decomposition, entropy concavity, and population convexity. -> Mixed-input population bound.
- **capacity** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:holevo): Bound the mean output by diagonal entropy and classical subadditivity, subtract the mixed-input bound, and attain the objective with products of the phase pair. -> Finite-block Holevo optimum and unassisted classical capacity.
- **partner** [claim](#stmt-cor-partner-additivity) / [proof](#sec:partners): Identify the conditional partner ensemble with the mean partner marginal, bound its Holevo information, and regularize after iterating the partner equality. -> Partner additivity for Holevo capacity, minimum output entropy, and classical capacity.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **pure_determinant**: The pure-input output determinant equals the completed-square expression in the excitation population.
- **partner_scope**: The partner retains its own regularization in the additive classical-capacity formula.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **grid_as_capacity**: Treat a finite scan of excitation populations as the exact channel capacity. -> A finite scan does not certify the maximum over the entire population interval.
- **partner_single_letter**: Replace the partner classical capacity by its one-shot Holevo capacity. -> The partner may require regularization even though the damping factor does not.
- **software_composition**: Transfer channel additivity to arbitrary interacting software components. -> No correspondence establishes the required quantum-channel objects and tensor-product structure.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The explicit pure-qubit determinant formula permits finite exact evaluation for rational channel parameters and excitation populations. This construction evaluates that formula; it does not certify the continuous capacity maximum or the entropy inequalities.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of rational-string triples gamma, nu, p; at most 125 triples.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each parameter lies in the closed unit interval; each token has at most 64 characters.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact pure-input output determinants as rational strings.
# TIME: Linear in the number of triples, with exact rational arithmetic costs.
# SPACE: Linear in the number of triples and rational bit lengths.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpq rational operations; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid shape, size, rational token, or parameter raises an exception.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq
rows = json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(rows, list) or not 1 <= len(rows) <= 125:
    raise ValueError('batch size')
answers = []
for row in rows:
    if not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != 3:
        raise ValueError('triple shape')
    if any(not isinstance(token, str) or not 1 <= len(token) <= 64 for token in row):
        raise ValueError('rational token')
    gamma, nu, p = [fmpq(token) for token in row]
    if any(value < 0 or value > 1 for value in (gamma, nu, p)):
        raise ValueError('parameter range')
    determinant = gamma*nu*(1-nu) + gamma*(1-gamma)*(p-nu)**2
    answers.append(str(determinant))
print(json.dumps(answers))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys as system\nimport json as serialization\nfrom fractions import Fraction as Rational\ntry:\n    inputs = serialization.loads(open(system.argv[1]).read())\n    supplied = serialization.loads(open(system.argv[2]).read())\n    prescribed = serialization.loads(system.argv[3])\n    if not isinstance(supplied, list) or len(supplied) != len(inputs):\n        raise ValueError('output shape')\n    actual = [Rational(v) for v in supplied]\n    wanted = [Rational(v) for v in prescribed]\n    certified = []\n    for triple in inputs:\n        damping, occupation, population = map(Rational, triple)\n        if not all(0 <= r <= 1 for r in (damping, occupation, population)):\n            raise ValueError('input range')\n        excited = (1-damping)*population + damping*occupation\n        coherence_squared = (1-damping)*population*(1-population)\n        minor = (1-excited)*excited - coherence_squared\n        if not 0 <= minor <= Rational(1,4):\n            raise ValueError('determinant range')\n        certified.append(minor)\n    if actual != certified or wanted != certified:\n        raise ValueError('determinant disagreement')\nexcept Exception:\n    system.exit(1)\nsystem.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact rational output determinants of pure qubit inputs with the specified excitation populations.",
    "reason": "The explicit pure-qubit determinant formula permits finite exact evaluation for rational channel parameters and excitation populations. This construction evaluates that formula; it does not certify the continuous capacity maximum or the entropy inequalities.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of rational-string triples gamma, nu, p; at most 125 triples.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Each parameter lies in the closed unit interval; each token has at most 64 characters.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact pure-input output determinants as rational strings.\n# TIME: Linear in the number of triples, with exact rational arithmetic costs.\n# SPACE: Linear in the number of triples and rational bit lengths.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpq rational operations; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: Invalid shape, size, rational token, or parameter raises an exception.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\nrows = json.load(sys.stdin)\nif not isinstance(rows, list) or not 1 <= len(rows) <= 125:\n    raise ValueError('batch size')\nanswers = []\nfor row in rows:\n    if not isinstance(row, list) or len(row) != 3:\n        raise ValueError('triple shape')\n    if any(not isinstance(token, str) or not 1 <= len(token) <= 64 for token in row):\n        raise ValueError('rational token')\n    gamma, nu, p = [fmpq(token) for token in row]\n    if any(value < 0 or value > 1 for value in (gamma, nu, p)):\n        raise ValueError('parameter range')\n    determinant = gamma*nu*(1-nu) + gamma*(1-gamma)*(p-nu)**2\n    answers.append(str(determinant))\nprint(json.dumps(answers))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The scalar capacity formula can support software that evaluates the specified quantum channel. This is an inferred implementation use; the paper proves no general software composition contract. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A simulation of qubit relaxation can use the stated population and coherence transformation. This conditional use does not establish a game mechanic or a general simulation guarantee. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A channel-analysis tool can expose the scalar Holevo objective and the attaining phase-pair ensemble. Numerical certification and a tool implementation are not supplied. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The reduction from unrestricted block ensembles to a scalar maximum supports specialized capacity-evaluation algorithms. The proof does not give a precision contract or an algorithmic complexity bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Density matrices and rectangular Gram factors are mathematical representations in the proof. A storage layout, indexing scheme or data-structure performance contract is ABSENT. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result describes a quantum channel and its information capacity. An accelerator architecture, execution mapping and hardware resource contract are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The operational result concerns unassisted classical communication. A secrecy criterion, adversary model and cryptographic protocol are ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scalar convexity fact concerns the output entropy of this channel. A learning objective, training procedure and statistical generalization contract are ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The channel capacity is a scalar maximum on a closed population interval. Concavity of binary entropy and convexity of the single-site output entropy make the stated objective concave, permitting a specialized continuous optimization formulation. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Tensor-product channel additivity is an information-theoretic statement. A distributed protocol, network interaction model and consensus contract are ABSENT. |
| other (quantum_information_theory) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | Quantum information theory is the direct application: the paper determines unassisted classical capacity and proves partner additivity for Holevo capacity, minimum output entropy and regularized classical capacity. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-model** (formal_scope): The capacity result concerns the specified qubit generalized amplitude-damping channel, with the stationary excited-state population convention fixed by its definition. Blocked use: ['Transferring the formula to a different noise channel without matching its population and coherence action.']
- **limit-assistance** (hypothesis): The operational capacity is unassisted and excludes preshared entanglement and feedback. Blocked use: ['Using this capacity as an entanglement-assisted or feedback-assisted capacity.']
- **limit-memory** (hypothesis): Repeated uses have the stated memoryless tensor-product action. Blocked use: ['Applying the block formula to correlated channel noise or channel memory.']
- **limit-decoding** (formal_scope): Product signal ensembles attain Holevo information, while the operational reliable-rate interpretation permits collective decoding. Blocked use: ['Treating the attaining ensemble as a supplied finite-length code or as a guarantee for separate output measurements.']
- **limit-partner** (hypothesis): Partner additivity requires a completely positive trace-preserving channel on nonzero finite-dimensional complex Hilbert spaces. Blocked use: ['Extending partner additivity to infinite-dimensional spaces or maps lacking the channel hypotheses.']
- **limit-partner-regularization** (formal_scope): Additivity with the damping factor does not remove the partner channel's own capacity regularization. Blocked use: ["Replacing the partner's regularized capacity by its one-shot Holevo capacity without another justification."]
- **limit-continuous-maximum** (nonconstructive): The attaining population is selected by a continuous scalar maximum. A finite exact optimizer, numerical precision contract and complexity guarantee are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Claiming that a sampled population is a certified maximizer.', 'Treating existence of the maximum as a finite exact capacity-evaluation algorithm.']
- **limit-entropy-units** (formal_scope): Capacity and Holevo information are in bits, while the entropy functions remain in nats. Blocked use: ['Combining entropy and capacity values without the stated conversion.']
- **limit-zero-weights** (edge_case): A vanishing block mass removes its weighted entropy contribution; it does not require division by that mass. Blocked use: ['Evaluating normalized block entropy by division at a vanishing block mass.']
- **limit-integral-trace** (hypothesis): The entropy-difference integral requires positive semidefinite matrices of equal trace; their sizes may differ. Blocked use: ['Using the integral identity for unequal traces or matrices outside the positive semidefinite domain.']
- **limit-logdet-domain** (hypothesis): The joint log-determinant convexity statement fixes the cross matrix and varies positive definite Hermitian matrix arguments. Blocked use: ['Allowing the cross matrix to vary while invoking the stated joint convexity.', 'Using the inverse and logarithm expression directly on singular arguments.']
- **limit-pure-output** (counterexample_scope): At positive damping and strictly interior stationary occupation, all channel outputs are positive definite. The pure-output criterion used for ordinary damping does not cover that regime. Blocked use: ['Justifying the thermal case solely by a pure-output criterion.']
- **limit-general-additivity** (counterexample_scope): The damping-channel additivity proof is specific to its channel structure; general Holevo additivity fails. Blocked use: ['Inferring Holevo additivity for arbitrary pairs of quantum channels.', 'Inferring general software composition or throughput laws from independent execution.']
- **limit-thermal-coefficients** (hypothesis): The thermal block inequality requires normalized Gram factors, nonnegative coefficients summing to one and the stated coefficient identity. Blocked use: ['Applying the thermal entropy bound to an arbitrary block state solely because its layout resembles the displayed matrix.']

Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\chi(\\mathcal A\\otimes\\mathcal N)\n &=\\chi(\\mathcal A)+\\chi(\\mathcal N),\\label{eq:partner-holevo}\\\\\n S_{\\min}(\\mathcal A\\otimes\\mathcal N)\n &=S_{\\min}(\\mathcal A)+S_{\\min}(\\mathcal N),\\label{eq:partner-min}\\\\\n C(\\mathcal A\\otimes\\mathcal N)\n &=C(\\mathcal A)+C(\\mathcal N).\\label{eq:partner-capacity}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $\\chi$ is the unconstrained one-shot Holevo capacity in <a href=\"#eq:holevo\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:holevo\">[eq:holevo]</a>, and $C$ is the regularized unassisted classical capacity in <a href=\"#eq:regularization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:regularization\">[eq:regularization]</a>.",

Suppose $\\alpha,\\beta,\\eta,\\delta\\ge0$ sum to one and $$\\label{eq:thermal-condition}\n \\alpha\\delta-\\beta\\eta=K^2,\\qquad K\\ge0.$$ Set $$\\label{eq:thermal-state}\n T=\\begin{pmatrix}\n \\alpha XX^*+\\beta YY^*&KXY^*\\\\\n KYX^*&\\eta XX^*+\\delta YY^*\n \\end{pmatrix},\\qquad\n U_0=\\alpha+\\beta,\\quad U_1=\\eta+\\delta,$$ and $u=U_0U_1-K^2$. Then $T$ is a state, $0\\le u\\le1/4$, and $$\\label{eq:thermal-bound}\n \\mathcal S(T)\\ge(\\alpha+\\eta)\\mathcal S(XX^*)\n             +(\\beta+\\delta)\\mathcal S(YY^*)+g(u).$$",
          "quote_sha256": "919560b1d189c080e4941fdac4ff2985e17813e37d9467ba0ae0249b2fc80e86"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "Normalized Gram factors and nonnegative normalized coefficients satisfying the cross-coefficient identity.",
      "question": "What entropy bound holds for thermal rectangular Gram blocks?",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-unital",
          "quote": "For every $\\gamma\\in[0,1]$, $$C(\\mathcal A_{\\gamma,1/2})\n =1-\\frac1{\\ln2}g(\\gamma/4)\n =1-\\frac1{\\ln2}h\\!\\left(\\frac{1+\\sqrt{1-\\gamma}}2\\right).$$ The pair $(\\lvert 0\\rangle\\pm\\lvert 1\\rangle)/\\sqrt2$ attains the one-use Holevo information, and its independent products attain every finite-power Holevo optimum.",

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 276 (`lean/docs/276.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Classical capacity and entropy inequalities for generalized amplitude damping](../../preprints/Classical-capacity-and-entropy-inequalities-for-generalized-amplitude-damping-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result determines the unassisted classical capacity of every generalized amplitude-damping channel with parameters in $[0,1]^2$. Its unrestricted Holevo information is additive across every number of repeated uses, equals an attained scalar maximum, and agrees with the operational capacity per use. Every maximizing equiprobable phase-pair product ensemble attains the corresponding block value. Additivity with an arbitrary different partner channel and decoding by separate measurements are not included.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

universe u

noncomputable section
open scoped BigOperators ComplexOrder
open Matrix Filter

namespace GAD

abbrev Basis (n : ℕ) := Fin n → Fin 2
abbrev QMatrix (n : ℕ) := Matrix (Basis n) (Basis n) ℂ

/-- The spectral definition of -Tr(P log P), with 0 log 0 = 0. -/
def entropy {ι : Type u} [Fintype ι] [DecidableEq ι] (P : Matrix ι ι ℂ) : ℝ :=
  (Matrix.trace (cfc Real.negMulLog P)).re

def IsState {ι : Type u} [Fintype ι] [DecidableEq ι] (P : Matrix ι ι ℂ) : Prop :=
  P.PosSemidef ∧ Matrix.trace P = 1


def kraus (γ ν : ℝ) (r : Fin 4) : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ :=
  if r = 0 then
    !![(Real.sqrt (1 - ν) : ℂ), 0;
       0, (Real.sqrt (1 - ν) * Real.sqrt (1 - γ) : ℂ)]
  else if r = 1 then
    !![0, (Real.sqrt (γ * (1 - ν)) : ℂ); 0, 0]
  else if r = 2 then
    !![(Real.sqrt ν * Real.sqrt (1 - γ) : ℂ), 0; 0, (Real.sqrt ν : ℂ)]
  else
    !![0, 0; (Real.sqrt (γ * ν) : ℂ), 0]

/-- Tensor products of the single-site Kraus operators, with the full Kraus index. -/
def tensorKraus (γ ν : ℝ) (n : ℕ) (r : Fin n → Fin 4) : QMatrix n :=
  fun i j ↦ ∏ k, kraus γ ν (r k) (i k) (j k)

/-- The memoryless n-fold generalized amplitude-damping channel. -/
def channel (γ ν : ℝ) (n : ℕ) (P : QMatrix n) : QMatrix n :=
  ∑ r : Fin n → Fin 4,
    tensorKraus γ ν n r * P * (tensorKraus γ ν n r).conjTranspose

/-- All finite ensembles, without any product or purity restriction. -/
structure Ensemble (n : ℕ) where
  size : ℕ
  weight : Fin size → ℝ
  weight_nonneg : ∀ a, 0 ≤ weight a
  weight_sum : ∑ a, weight a = 1
  signal : Fin size → QMatrix n
  signal_state : ∀ a, IsState (signal a)

def ensembleValue (γ ν : ℝ) {n : ℕ} (E : Ensemble n) : ℝ :=
  (entropy (∑ a, (E.weight a : ℂ) • channel γ ν n (E.signal a)) -
    ∑ a, E.weight a * entropy (channel γ ν n (E.signal a))) / Real.log 2


def holevo (γ ν : ℝ) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  sSup (Set.range (ensembleValue γ ν : Ensemble n → ℝ))

/-- An unrestricted n-use quantum code with one collective output POVM. -/
structure Code (n : ℕ) where
  messages : ℕ
  messages_pos : 0 < messages
  encoding : Fin messages → QMatrix n
  encoding_state : ∀ a, IsState (encoding a)
  decoding : Fin messages → QMatrix n
  decoding_pos : ∀ a, (decoding a).PosSemidef
  decoding_sum : ∑ a, decoding a = 1

def averageError (γ ν : ℝ) {n : ℕ} (C : Code n) : ℝ :=
  1 - (∑ a, (Matrix.trace (C.decoding a * channel γ ν n (C.encoding a))).re) /
    (C.messages : ℝ)

/-- The liminf rate condition written as its eventual lower-bound characterization.
There is no preshared entanglement, feedback, or restriction on encodings/decoding. -/
def Achievable (γ ν R : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ C : (n : ℕ) → Code n,
    Tendsto (fun n ↦ averageError γ ν (C n)) atTop (nhds 0) ∧
    ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∀ᶠ n : ℕ in atTop,
      R - ε ≤ Real.log ((C n).messages : ℝ) / Real.log 2 / (n : ℝ)

/-- Operational unassisted classical capacity, not a definition by regularization. -/
def capacity (γ ν : ℝ) : ℝ := sSup {R : ℝ | Achievable γ ν R}

def g (u : ℝ) : ℝ := Real.binEntropy ((1 + Real.sqrt (1 - 4 * u)) / 2)

def v (γ ν p : ℝ) : ℝ := γ * ν * (1 - ν) + γ * (1 - γ) * (p - ν) ^ 2

def objective (γ ν p : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  Real.binEntropy ((1 - γ) * p + γ * ν) - g (v γ ν p)

def Maximizes (γ ν p : ℝ) : Prop :=
  p ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 ∧ ∀ q ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1, objective γ ν q ≤ objective γ ν p


def phaseSignal (p : ℝ) (s b : Fin 2) : ℂ :=
  if b = 0 then (Real.sqrt (1 - p) : ℂ)
  else if s = 0 then (Real.sqrt p : ℂ) else -(Real.sqrt p : ℂ)

def phaseProduct (p : ℝ) {n : ℕ} (s : Basis n) (i : Basis n) : ℂ :=
  ∏ k, phaseSignal p (s k) (i k)

def phaseState (p : ℝ) {n : ℕ} (s : Basis n) : QMatrix n :=
  fun i j ↦ phaseProduct p s i * star (phaseProduct p s j)

/-- Holevo value of the explicit 2^n equiprobable product signals. -/
def phaseValue (γ ν p : ℝ) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (entropy (∑ s : Basis n, (((2 : ℝ) ^ n)⁻¹ : ℂ) • channel γ ν n (phaseState p s)) -
    ∑ s : Basis n, ((2 : ℝ) ^ n)⁻¹ * entropy (channel γ ν n (phaseState p s))) /
    Real.log 2

theorem main (γ ν : ℝ) (hγ : γ ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1)
    (hν : ν ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) :
    (∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n → holevo γ ν n = (n : ℝ) * holevo γ ν 1) ∧
    (∃ p : ℝ, Maximizes γ ν p ∧
      ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n → holevo γ ν n = (n : ℝ) / Real.log 2 * objective γ ν p) ∧
    capacity γ ν = holevo γ ν 1 ∧
    (∀ p : ℝ, Maximizes γ ν p → ∀ n : ℕ, 1 ≤ n →
      (∀ s : Basis n, IsState (phaseState p s)) ∧
      holevo γ ν n = phaseValue γ ν p n) := by
  sorry

end GAD
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We determine the unassisted classical capacity of every qubit generalized amplitude-damping channel, for all damping strengths and thermal occupations. It equals the one-shot Holevo capacity and is given by a one-variable maximum. A binary pure-state ensemble attains the one-use optimum, and its independent products attain the optimum at every block length. We also prove that one-shot Holevo capacity, minimum output entropy, and regularized unassisted classical capacity are additive under tensor product with every finite-dimensional completely positive trace-preserving partner channel.

| [stmt-prop-pure-bound](#stmt-prop-pure-bound) | proposition | `prop:pure-bound` | 12 | For every $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, put $\mathcal A=\mathcal A_{\gamma,\nu}$ and $e(p)=g(v_{\gamma,\nu}(p))$. |
| [stmt-cor-mixed-bound](#stmt-cor-mixed-bound) | corollary | `cor:mixed-bound` | 13 | For every $\gamma,\nu\in[0,1]$, integer $n\ge1$, and density matrix $\rho$ on $(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes n}$, let $p_j$ be its excitation probability at site $j$. |
